# D-007 + D-008 (MERGED) — the GPU program system: one IR for every shader, one device

> **This is the single master doc for the shader/kernel detour.** It merges the two former detours — **D-007** (CKIR
> becomes the universal shader IR) and **D-008** (the gpu-context convergence: one device, one program surface) — into ONE
> ordered subslice table. The old two-doc split made the sequence unreadable (two different "C3"s, interleaved B/C slices);
> this replaces both. `D-008-gpu-context-convergence.md` is now a stub pointing here.

- **North-star ADRs:** [0101](../decisions/0101-ir-is-source-of-truth-for-all-shaders.md) (the IR is the single source of
  truth) · [0102](../decisions/0102-render-data-lighting-pass-architecture.md) (how the IR feeds the renderer) ·
  [0103](../decisions/0103-gpu-context-owns-every-gpu-program.md) (gpu-context owns every GPU program; supersedes 0099 §6).
  Corpus: `docs/systems/shader-ir-corpus-and-stages.md`.

## The mission (why the IR must be frontier-complete)

Compute the best + most interesting things end to end — **ML/AI · FFT · simulations · skinning · particles · light
systems · rendering that out-beauties Unreal · ray tracing · beautiful + stylized (toon/NPR) effects · anything a GPU can
do** — portably across ALL backends, correct + performant + bit-exact. One IR (CKIR) is the single source; GLSL / HLSL /
WGSL / MSL / CUDA / HIP are derived OUTPUTS, never authored or stored. This detour hardens that IR + the device surface it
runs on; the actual render pipelines / physics / editor are built ON it in their own phases. **No compromises — the
frontier version of every capability, by construction.**

## The two invariants (grep-gated, not prose)

> **No module outside a backend names a shading language or a bytecode.**
> **I1** — GLSL/HLSL/WGSL/MSL/CUDA source lives only inside one backend TU, between our emitter and the vendor compiler.
> **I2** — SPIR-V/DXIL/PTX bytes never appear in a public header; a consumer holds an opaque `IGpuProgram`, nothing else.

Currency **in** = the IR (`KGraph` + `KEntry`). Currency **out** = `IGpuProgram`. **Both are CLOSED** (C0…C2): the
`crd-no-shader-language-leak` gate is green with an empty allowlist, and `ShaderModule`/raw-SPIR-V is gone from every public
header. One `VkDevice` — the `VulkanGpuContext` owns it; `rhi-vulkan` adopts, never creates.

## ⭐⭐ Legacy path vs CKIR path — where we are, where we LAND (user 2026-07-12, "very important")

The engine is **mid-migration**, and there are TWO shader/render paths in the tree right now. This is deliberate and temporary:

- **LEGACY path (the shipped runtime today):** the runtime renderer + the `runtime/examples/smoke_*.cpp` render through
  **rhi** pipelines with the **hand-authored GLSL Effect system** — `reflect_triangle.vert`/`.frag` source files → `crd::shader::EffectDesc`
  → `runtime->create_effect` → `device->create_program(cooked SPIR-V)`. GLSL is written BY HAND here.
- **CKIR path (this detour, built in `tests/gpu-context-*` first):** everything is authored in **backend-neutral CKIR**
  (`KGraph`+`KEntry`); GLSL/HLSL/WGSL/MSL/PTX appear ONLY as **emitter output inside a gpu-context backend** — never
  hand-written, never stored. `create_program(KGraph,KEntry)` → `IRasterContext`/`IComputeContext` → the GPU.

What is **already unified**: the **device seam** (C2/D-008). The runtime bootstraps a `create_vulkan_gpu_context(...)` and rhi
**adopts** its `VkDevice` — ONE device, gpu-context-owned. What is **not yet migrated**: the runtime's actual **rendering** still
runs on rhi pipelines + the GLSL Effect system; it does not yet route draws/dispatches through the gpu-context contexts + CKIR.

Two more "cook"-named things that are NOT the B8-k cook: **D1** serializes the CKIR/material graph into the versioned **`.crdr`**
resource (replacing stored GLSL/HLSL text); **D2** is the offline **cook** (IR → SPIR-V/DXIL/MSL/WGSL/PTX bytecode, content-hash
cached). The **B8-k "cook seam"** is the in-memory material→per-pass-program assembly that FEEDS D1/D2 — it touches no `.crdr` yet.

> **⛔ THE ENDGAME (non-negotiable): after this detour is FULLY over, the engine is 100% CKIR.** The runtime renderer migrates
> ENTIRELY onto CKIR → gpu-context (`IRasterContext`/`IComputeContext`) + the `.crdr` cook (D1–D5). The legacy GLSL Effect
> system (`crd::shader::EffectDesc`, `create_effect`, the hand-authored `.vert`/`.frag` files, the SPIR-V-source path) is
> **DELETED** — no dual path survives. GLSL/HLSL/etc. remain ONLY as machine-emitted backend outputs behind I1/I2. The
> transitional coexistence above exists solely so the shipped engine keeps rendering while the CKIR path is hardened; it is a
> bridge, not a destination. **Full CKIR, no exceptions, once the detour lands.**

## ▶ The locked order (user 2026-07-11): FULL visual frontier FIRST, then hesap-GPU

The compute substrate hesap-GPU needs is **already proven** (GEMM at cuBLAS parity, 6 backends, bit-exact) — so hesap is a
dependency-light return whenever wanted. The user chose to complete the **entire** visual frontier first anyway. hesap-GPU
is the LAST stop of this detour, not an early exit. Two side tracks per slice: **IR** = the shader/emitter side (was
D-007); **DEV** = the device/context side (was D-008).

> **⭐ Render→compute dependencies are built DURING this detour (user 2026-07-12).** CKIR *is* the GPU compute compiler, so
> the compute PRIMITIVES the frontier rendering needs — **FFT** (convolution/physically-accurate bloom + glare · FFT ocean),
> GPU **sort** (Gaussian-splat depth · OIT · light lists), **scan / stream-compaction** (clustered light-cull · Nanite
> cluster-cull · particle compaction), **reduction** (auto-exposure histogram · SDSM / Hi-Z depth min-max · luminance), small
> **GEMM / MLP** (neural radiance cache · neural materials, w/ B10 coop-vectors) — are implemented here as CKIR compute kernels
> (bit-exact CPU oracle + both backends) as part of the slice that needs them. This serves rendering now AND hesap-GPU later
> from one source. The BROADER hesap-GPU (dense/sparse LA, eigensolvers, ODE, stats — the parts rendering never calls) stays
> post-detour. Each gap slice below flags its compute dependency where one exists.

The kernel was right, the gate was wrong; it now measures POPPING directly (dithered vs undithered sweep across a quantisation boundary). ⛔ Found + fixed: KOp::Exp2 was missing from BOTH compute emitters (Log2 present) so the kernel would not emit at all — THIRD occurrence of the compute-emitter-lag scar, which is why an emitter COMPLETENESS GATE now exists. ⭐ ALSO LANDED: per-strand styling (curl amp/freq/PHASE/length from buffers) which removed the ribbon/noodle look and collapsed 903 shader compiles per frame to 4; whole-groom single-dispatch batching; 2x supersampling — the fix for hair reading as LINE ART, since a deferred G-buffer shades ONE of ~148 strands overlapping each pixel. ⭐ RESEARCH BASE: `docs/research/2026-07-19-hair-fur-frontier-collection.md` (9-paper dossier: Chiang16 · Huang22 · Yan17 · Zinke08 DS · **Hu TOG-07/2026 volumetric MS** · Yuksel08 DOM · EGSR25 aggregated-BCSDF LOD · Lipp26 deferred SW raster · Yuksel24 hair meshes · Curly-Cue 24) + `2026-07-19-huang-microfacet-hair.md`; papers in `docs/research/papers/hair_fur/`. → **B18-f RT STRAND TIER (the C3/B9 LSS closeout):** LSS curve/sphere AS build (C3) + curve-hit attributes in the RT closest-hit (B9) + path-traced hair reference (the BCSDF as CHIT + dual scattering) — **DELIVERED (2026-07-20) — C3 (#28) and B9 (#29) both flipped ✅.** New `ckir_lss.hpp`: the analytic ray/round-cone `lss_intersect`, conservative per-segment AABBs, a host-unrolled reference sweep, and `build_rt_hair_shade_kernel` — hardware traversal feeding the **B18-a Chiang BCSDF**, with the fibre frame rebuilt from the hit segment's tangent and h taken as the signed perpendicular miss distance in radius units (exact by construction; a normal-derived h degenerates at grazing angles). Shading is an INLINE-ray-query compute kernel, which is the same architecture every other integrator in this detour uses (RT-3/4/5, IB-1/2/3) — the RT-PIPELINE route would additionally need procedural hit groups (`KStage::Intersection` emission + an SBT intersection record), which the engine does not implement today. **Gates:** `[lss]` 8/8 (6740 assertions); Vulkan 256 rays / 36 segments / 45 hits, ZERO hit/miss disagreements vs the oracle, maxabs 8.3e-05; DX12 the same scene/seed/oracle; and the shading gate checks the IR fibre frame against a SECOND plain-C++ frame construction sharing no code with it, agreeing to <2e-5. **⛔⛔ Scars:** the round cone had FOUR independent defects, all invisible for a capsule (rr==0) — 118 of 132 reported hits were off-surface against a ray-march ground truth; `rayQueryGenerateIntersectionEXT` needs tHit inside the ray's CURRENT (narrowing) range; `eval_cpu_kernel` is SCALAR and silently evaluated Vec3/Swizzle to garbage (now asserts); and a `tmax·rlen·(1/rlen)` round trip made a MISS test as nearer than the caller's bound, clobbering the accumulated u with 0. |

MAX + compare are order-independent ⇒ **BIT-EXACT** vs the CPU oracle. The `[hzb]` test builds a 16×16 depth split (near wall left / far background right) → the pyramid → 3 clusters: behind-the-wall CULLED, over-open-background VISIBLE, in-FRONT-of-wall VISIBLE — GPU == oracle == analytic on Vulkan (8 asserts) + DX12 (7); a template `make_pipe` factory drives the multi-pass dispatch from both backends. 5-backend emit gate (12). mip selection is trivial host-side cull-setup (findMSB of the footprint; the GPU does the data-parallel HZB sample + occlusion decision). | — | — | — | — | **══════ REN·B — the INTERACTIVE FRONTIER: UI-AS-ENTITIES on the crd-scene ECS · the RNA-class property system · our own TEXT + VECTOR stacks · docking/tear-off · the five flagship widgets · the editor-shell capstone. EXPANDED 2026-07-24 (user: "we must be able to make ANY program with this engine and we must do them beautifully" — editor with game-engine + Blender + Cascadeur + DAW + CAD/CAM abilities, the hesap MATLAB environment, every future Cerid application). Research: `docs/research/2026-07-24-ui-frontier-ren-band.md` (the survey + recipe). THE ARCHITECTURE (4 decisions user-locked interactively): **① UI IS ECS ENTITIES in a DEDICATED UI WORLD per window** — same crd-scene machinery (archetypes · relations · change detection · command buffers), different World instance, so the editor's interface never lives inside the document it edits; change detection IS the dirty-layout/paint invalidation; öbek/SCEN IS layout persistence ("record layouts / user preferences"); command buffers ARE transactional edits + undo; the agent layer composes UI like it composes scenes (a differentiator no other engine has); widget-granularity entities (a text field is ONE entity — glyphs are paint output, never entities). **② crd-reflect — the RNA-class PROPERTY SYSTEM is the centerpiece**: every reflected property is inspectable/bindable/animatable/undoable/agent-drivable — Blender's actual superpower, generalized. **③ COMPUTE-FIRST vector graphics (Vello-class), CKIR-authored, CPU-oracle-gated.** **④ THREE authoring surfaces**: C++ builder + UI-as-data (öbek) + reactive bindings (no DSL — ADR-0081 C++-only doctrine). Paint = batched CKIR primitives + a custom-CKIR-material escape-hatch widget; the ONE curve engine (hesap-interp) is UI animation's engine (4th consumer; ⭐ user-flagged "very important"); crd-imgui stays the debug overlay; crd-ui is the PRODUCT UI; REN-17 mints the crd-ui ADR superseding ADR-0023 in place. Honest sizing: REN-14 (shaper) and REN-15 (vector) are each month-class — named, scheduled, never denied. ══════** |
| ⬜ | 113 | REN-10 | DEV | **the ECS AUDIT + the UI-WORLD substrate (the data-driven reconfiguration the UI shape demands)** — close the four UI-shaped gaps in crd-scene BEFORE building on it: **(a) ORDERED child relations** (deterministic sibling order — layout order AND z-order demand it; audit ADR-0050's relation storage for stable ordering + cheap reorder); **(b) observers/reactivity** (audit the deferred event-stream callbacks for property-change observation fan-out); **(c) RUNTIME-registered component types** (plugins/agents defining new property-bearing components without recompiling — the data-driven mandate); **(d) World lifecycle + CROSS-WORLD references** (generation-checked handles — a viewport widget referencing a scene World must FAIL CLOSED when that World dies; N UI Worlds + M document Worlds coexisting, per-World tick schedules). Gate: ordered children survive insert/remove/reorder deterministically; a runtime-registered component round-trips through SCEN; a cross-World handle detects its target World's death; the existing 35k-assert scene suite stays green (zero regression — this is an AUDIT, not a rewrite). |

A material may only read the surface contract; a technique only `SurfaceData` + its declared bindings; neither sees the other. **⭐ THE BINDING CONTRACT** (load-bearing): technique DECLARES pass-frequency inputs by name+type, the pass DECLARES `reads`, the COOKER VERIFIES — mismatch = named cook-time rejection, and bindings are assigned from the set-frequency model (0 frame · 1 pass · 2 material · 3 object), killing the hand-picked-slot fight. **⭐⭐ ÜBERSHADER vs VARIANTS — our IR dissolves the trade:** `specialize_variant`+`lower_entry` reduce ONE authored übergraph to an optimal static variant, so SHIP mode specializes every DECLARED combination + dedups by content hash while EDITOR mode emits the SAME graph unspecialized with options as UNIFORM branches (coherent ⇒ near-free) for instant iteration — one artifact, two lowering levels (a second authoring path would be the real mistake). **⭐ THE FREE COLLAPSE:** for `PassType::Shadow` `n_out=0` and the surface is never consumed ⇒ `lower_entry` DCEs the ENTIRE surface computation ⇒ every opaque material cooks to the SAME empty shadow FS and dedups to ONE variant; what engines hand-engineer as "depth-only permutations" falls out of lowering+content-hash because we compose in an IR, not text. **INDUSTRY GROUNDING (2025-26):** Slang modules/interfaces/generics = specialization without preprocessor string-pasting, compile-to-IR + link (validates the CKIR approach) · UE5 **Substrate** replaces fixed shading models with modular slabs + operators, with *parameter blending* merging slabs to need ONE lighting evaluation · Filament splits material(surface) from engine(light/shadow/BRDF) and ships `variantFilter` so an app DECLARES variants it will never need · Frostbite/RPS/Activision: persistent resources are IMPORTED and EXCLUDED from aliasing (TAA history, shadow atlas) · RPS **subprograms** = subgraph composition + `[subgraph(atomic,sequential)]` scheduling attributes · Unity URP **injection points** + Renderer Features let a user add a pass WITHOUT editing the base graph. **THE THREE CAPABILITIES THIS SLICE ADDS TO THE ASSET (user-directed):** (a) **PERSISTENT + PING-PONG resources** — `kind = "persistent_image"` / `"pingpong_image"`, excluded from transient aliasing, `$prev`/`$curr` addressing — the substrate for blur chains, TAA history, SSR/DDGI/ReSTIR temporal reuse and auto-exposure; (b) **SUBGRAPHS** — a graph may `include` another by name with parameter binding, so techniques compose instead of being copy-pasted; (c) **INJECTION POINTS** — a graph declares named anchors (`@after_opaque`, `@before_post`) and a separate asset injects passes there, so a user inserts a pass BETWEEN existing nodes without forking the base graph. ✅ **37.2 + 37.3 + 37.4 + 37.5 LANDED 2026-07-26, BOTH BACKENDS.** **37.2** `ckir_technique.hpp` + the new `crd-technique-cook` module: a technique is a NAMED graph function with a DECLARED contract, and it has **TWO PROVENANCES, ONE CONTRACT** — a registered `TechniqueBody` (engine/plugin C++) OR an **AUTHORED serialized CKIR graph SPLICED IN at cook time** (no engine code, no recompile, which is what makes the top rule reachable for the SHADER half). `splice_graph` inlines a deserialized graph by ABI index, remapping struct ids + the variadic ext pool, and REJECTS (never partially splices) a blob carrying kernel statements — a fragment technique is a VALUE EXPRESSION by construction and a dropped statement would be a miscompile. `.crdt` = parse + 12 named rejections + lossless emit + canonical cook; VALUE bindings (`csm_light_vp`, `csm_map_size`) are ENGINE STATE, not graph resources — listing them in `reads` would put non-resources into the dependency graph and the cooker would reject them as never-written; they are checked one layer down by the renderer resolver, which fails `init_programs` by name. Plus the **frame-frequency CAMERA POSITION** (header 96..98 via `camera_position_from_vp`, the SAME exact reconstruction the cascade fit uses so the two cannot disagree) — `view_dir` was the placeholder constant (0,1,0) until now, which degenerates NoV and with it the Smith term, `env_brdf_approx` and energy compensation. **⭐⭐ 37.4 CSM IS AN AUTHORED TECHNIQUE AND THE DELETION IS THE PROOF:** `build_scene_fs_shadowed` (~120 lines of hand-written cascade select + PCF + slope-scaled bias) is **GONE**; so are `build_scene_vs_textured`/`build_scene_fs_textured` (the textured variant is the SAME cooked material with `textured = true` over the ONE shared VS). **There is no hand-written fragment shader left in `scene_renderer.cpp`.** Device gate: one scene, one camera, three techniques BY NAME — `standard_forward` gives a lit gradient, `unlit` draws the same geometry with materially different pixels, `forward_csm` darkens under a caster and NEVER brightens; ⛔ plus the assertion that makes the other three mean anything — **an unresolved technique name FAILS `init_programs`** rather than falling back, because a plausible frame rendered with the WRONG technique is indistinguishable from a correct one and a typo would otherwise read as a pass. **37.5 PERSISTENT + PING-PONG, both backends:** `create_persistent_image(key, desc)` + `persistent_image_was_live(key)` (appended at the vtable END, D135) — keyed by a stable identity, excluded from transient aliasing AND from the retire queue, contents + layout/state carried across `reset()`; a RESIZE recreates and reports `was_live == false` (a differently-shaped history is WORSE than none — reprojection would read plausible-looking garbage). Ping-pong = two keys + an executor-owned swap, deliberately NOT a new device concept, so no author ever hand-manages a frame-parity bit. ⭐ **THE DESIGN DEFECT PERSISTENCE EXPOSED, REMOVED RATHER THAN DOCUMENTED:** `ImageNode::transient` was ONE bool standing in for **THREE INDEPENDENT QUESTIONS** — is it GRAPH-OWNED (RTT barrier semantics + whether the end-of-frame readback applies; a borrowed wrapper has NO readback buffer), is it ALIASABLE (the transient pool / retire queue / free path), and is its state FRAME-LOCAL (whether the frame-start reset applies). For a transient all three answers are yes and for an import all three are no, so one bool served perfectly — right up until a resource that is graph-owned, NOT aliasable and NOT frame-local. Every site that read `transient` meant exactly one of the three and silently got the other two wrong (a SEGFAULT handing a borrowed target to `frame_readback`, an RTT barrier taken down the imported path, and a frame-start reset wiping deliberately-carried state). **THE FIX IS STRUCTURAL, BOTH BACKENDS:** `enum class Own {Imported, Transient, Persistent}` + named predicates `graph_owned()` / `aliasable()`. There is no `n.transient` any more, so a site cannot ask the wrong question by accident and a fourth ownership kind cannot inherit a previous kind’s answers. ⭐ **The third question has NO predicate ON PURPOSE — it is answered by WHERE THE STATE LIVES:** a persistent image’s live layout (VK) / resource state (DX12) is stored in the REGISTRY ENTRY, reached only through `live_layout()`/`live_state()`; the frame-start reset writes the NODE’s fields, which for a persistent node are dead. So the "skip persistent" exception no longer exists (it cannot reach that state), the end-of-frame WRITE-BACK no longer exists (one home, nothing to fall out of sync), and a transition FROM `UNDEFINED` — which the spec permits to DISCARD CONTENTS — is unreachable by construction. Two conditional exceptions and a synchronisation step replaced by ONE accessor: what was a rule to remember is now a property of the layout. **Verified:** Vulkan 3621/210 · DX12 1180/118 · scene-render 467/14 · technique-cook 45/4. ✅ **37.6 SUBGRAPHS + INJECTION POINTS LANDED 2026-07-26.** `flatten_frame_graph` (new `frame_compose.cpp`) inlines every `[[include]]` and splices every `[[inject]]`, then runs the **SAME validator a hand-authored graph faces** — ⭐ the same decision REN-36.3 made for `for_each`, for the same reason: composition that reached the scheduler would need a special case in lifetime analysis, barriers, aliasing AND the dependency sort. It reaches none of them. `[[include]] graph/as/bind`: ⛔ `as` NAMESPACING IS LOAD-BEARING, not cosmetic — two instances of one subgraph would otherwise both declare `scratch` and both WRITE it, which NO validator flags; the picture just comes out *almost* right. Nesting compounds (`vp.main.bloom.*`), so two viewports each running a bloom chain stay disjoint all the way down. `bind` rewrites a subgraph name into the includer scope; ⭐ `@`-prefixed names are EXTERNAL SENTINELS, not graph resources (`@output` always was; a subgraph `@input` is the same idea), and a graph WITH includes is legitimately PARTIAL — `NoOutputPass`/`ResourceNeverWritten` defer to the flattened result, which is what lets a subgraph be authored and shipped ON ITS OWN. `[[anchor]]`+`[[inject]]`: ⛔ anchors are DECLARED, so the base-graph author states where extension is safe — that is the whole difference between an extension point and a monkey-patch; the anchor decides where a pass is INSERTED, the dependency sort still decides where it EXECUTES. 7 new named rejections; blob v3; the editor round-trip stays BYTE-lossless with composition included. ⛔ **Finding worth keeping:** the gate asset first had the blur read+write `hdr` IN PLACE and the cycle detector rejected it — correctly: an in-place ping-pong IS a dependency cycle, and resolving it is exactly what 37.5 persistent/ping-pong exists for. **Verified:** frame-cook 196/10 · Vulkan frame-graph 255/19 · DX12 frame-graph 177/11 · scene-render 494/15. ✅ **37.7 VARIANT MATRIX + DUAL MODE LANDED 2026-07-26** (`ckir_variant.hpp`). The matrix is **DECLARED, NOT DISCOVERED** — every axis comes from something an author wrote (a technique `TechniqueOption` range, a material `ShadingModel` tag, the passes a frame graph asks for) — plus Filament `variantFilter` as a first-class NEGATIVE declaration applied BEFORE cooking, so a filtered combination costs nothing at all (not cook time, not memory, not a hash). ⛔ **TWO HASHES, DELIBERATELY DISTINCT:** `variant_key_hash` LOOKS A PROGRAM UP; `graph_content_hash` (over the canonical padding-free serialization of the **LOWERED** graph) decides what IS the same program — two different keys legitimately share content (that is dedup working), so conflating them would be a collision that silently drops a program. ⭐ **THE FREE COLLAPSE, MEASURED rather than asserted** (`dedup_ratio()`): a flat material and a texture-sampling one produce BIT-IDENTICAL `Shadow` programs while their `Forward` programs differ, `DepthPrepass` collapses onto the SAME program (one program for every opaque material across BOTH depth-only passes), and swapping the TECHNIQUE cannot change it — a technique is never invoked there. ⭐ **SHAPE vs BRANCH options:** a tap count UNROLLS a different number of samples and no uniform expresses that, so `VariantAxis::shape` says which axes the editor übershader can genuinely cover and which it pins at their default and recooks — stating it is the difference between a dual mode and a claim. ⛔ **One API defect found and REMOVED, not documented:** `cook_variant_matrix` originally took `SurfaceInputs` + binding node ids BY VALUE, but a CKIR node id indexes ONE graph and the matrix cooks each variant into a FRESH one — passing ids across graphs made the builders walk operands that meant something else (the first run HUNG). The signature now takes a `VariantEnvFn` the matrix calls WITH THE GRAPH IT IS ABOUT TO FILL, so a cross-graph id is not expressible. **Verified:** kir `[variant]` 57/5. ✅ **37.8 + 37.9 LANDED 2026-07-26 — REN-37 IS COMPLETE.** `viewport.hpp`/`viewport.cpp` in `crd-frame-cook` + the RENDERER SPLIT. **`SceneRenderer::contribute(fg, ...)` RECORDS WITHOUT OWNING**; `render()` is now exactly `contribute()` plus reset/build/execute, so the single- and multi-viewport paths CANNOT DRIFT. **Gated on device: TWO viewports of different sizes from different cameras, both rendered, `last_submit_count() == 1`.** `compose_frame` assembles ONE `FrameGraphDesc` whose `[[include]]`s are the active viewports — each namespaced by its id, each bound to its own output, AT MOST ONE `present` (two would each blit over the other and the winner would depend on declaration order — flicker that points nowhere); a `shared` graph is included ONCE outside any viewport so two viewports reading the shadow atlas order behind a single producer for free. `select_viewports` admits `EveryFrame` viewports FIRST and unconditionally (⛔ the main viewport is never starved by thumbnails — non-negotiable, which is why it is a separate pass and not a priority value), then fills the remaining budget in **measured GPU ms fed by REN-8** by priority **then AGE** — ⭐ the ageing term is what prevents permanent starvation of one thumbnail; deferred viewports stay DIRTY and are REPORTED (a silent cap reads as "everything rendered"); a settled thumbnail grid costs **ZERO** viewport work. Invalidation is **DECLARED, not hand-set** (`depends_on(Asset|Material|Camera|Resize)`) because the failure mode of a hand-set flag is a STALE THUMBNAIL, which looks exactly like a correct one. ⛔ **The split exposed a genuine DANGLING-POINTER bug, fixed STRUCTURALLY:** `add_pass(...).execute(fn, user)` stores the user pointer and dereferences it at `execute()` — which only worked because `render()` executed in the SAME CALL, keeping its LOCALS alive. Separate recording from execution and every such local dangles (it SEGFAULTED immediately). The per-contribution draw list and state now live in a CONTRIBUTION ARENA **reserved to its exact capacity in the constructor**, so no `push_back` can reallocate and move an entry the graph already points at — the same discipline REN-36.3’s expansion table uses — and the cap is CHECKED (exceeding it reports zero draws rather than corrupting memory). `begin_frame()` recycles the arena; `render()` does it for you. ✅ **37.10 THE INTEGRATION LANDED 2026-07-26 — THE RENDERER NOW *HOSTS* AN AUTHORED GRAPH INSTEAD OF BUILDING PASSES.** ⛔ Until this, REN-37 had built the whole stack and proved every layer on device while `scene_renderer.cpp` still hand-built its cascade + scene passes in C++, carrying its own violation notice — the SAME defect 37.1 was created to fix (`build_fs_for_pass` referenced only by tests). **The ~50-line C++ pass block is DELETED**; the identical frame is now `assets/frame/forward_csm.frame.toml` recorded through `FrameRecorder`, with the renderer supplying only what a graph CANNOT know (the target, the resolved draw lists, the cascade count, the per-cascade programs). Cascade count, atlas size/format, pass order, what casts, what receives and WHICH TECHNIQUE shades it are all asset text now. **(a) `FrameRecorder`** — `execute_frame_graph` had the same ownership defect one layer down (it CREATED its own graph per call), so it is now `record()` + create/build/execute and a host can record N authored graphs into ONE graph; its per-recording arena is reserved to its exact capacity because `execute(fn, user)` stores POINTERS the host dereferences long after `record()` returned. **(b) ⭐ REN-36.3-b CLOSED:** the asset’s `all`/`any`/`none` filters are EVALUATED — `World::component_id_by_name` (trailing-identifier match over the decorated `typeid` name) + `has_component_id`; ⛔ an UNKNOWN component name matches NOTHING, because "I could not resolve this filter" must never silently mean "this filter passes". Evaluated per GROUP (O(groups), not O(instances)) against the group’s representative entity. **(c) ⭐ SHADOWS ON/OFF IS A CAPABILITY TIER, not an `if`:** `forward_csm` declares `requires = ["shadows"]` + `fallback = "crd://frame/forward_basic"` (new asset — no atlas, no cascade passes, `standard_forward` instead), so turning shadows off costs NOTHING rather than running the machinery and multiplying by one, and the step-down is REPORTED. **(d) REN-37.7 IN THE RENDERER:** every FS goes through `cook_fs` — cook, lower, CONTENT-HASH, reuse — so the free collapse becomes real work saved rather than a claim; the FS members are BORROWED from the cache that owns them. **(e) PER-DRAW TEXTURES** (`DrawItem::texture`, appended at END): a draw’s own map beats the pass’s sampled read, which removes the interim state where a textured group LOST its albedo the instant shadows turned on. **(f) THE WHOLE LOOP GATED:** `ViewportRegistry` → `select_viewports` → N x `contribute()` → ONE build, ONE submission — three viewports in one submit, all three targets verified rendered, then the settled frame collapses to the live view alone and invalidating one asset brings back EXACTLY its thumbnail. **FINAL VERIFICATION:** scene-render **537/17** · frame-cook 267/13 · Vulkan 3621/210 (frame-graph 241/17) · DX12 1180/118 (frame-graph 177/11) · technique-cook 45/4 · kir 52,881/257 — ALL GREEN. **INCREMENTS:** **37.1** renderer consumes cooked material variants (replaces the hand-written `build_scene_*`; set-frequency bindings) — nothing else is possible until this lands → **37.2** technique as an authored `.crdt` asset (`build_fs_for_pass` calls the named technique; port `shade_forward` as `standard_forward`) → **37.3** the binding contract + cook-time verification → **37.4** CSM as an authored technique (DELETE the hand-written shadowed FS + the C++ cascade passes — the deletion is the proof) → **37.5** persistent/ping-pong resources + a blur chain → **37.6** subgraphs + injection points → **37.7** variant matrix + dual mode (declared key, cook specialization, D3 dedup, editor übershader) → **37.8** THE FRAME-LEVEL GRAPH + viewport model → **37.9** on-demand + budgeted viewport scheduling. ✅ **37.1 LANDED 2026-07-26.** The renderer cooks BOTH its Forward and Shadow programs through `build_fs_for_pass`; the shadowed FS is unified on the same OpenPBR surface + Cook-Torrance BRDF x visibility; the toy `build_scene_vs`/`build_scene_fs` (`0.25 + 0.75*N.L`) are **DELETED** — clang-tidy flagging them unused is the proof the cooked path took over. ⛔⛔ **THE SCAR THAT BLOCKED IT (root-caused, fixed, gated):** B7 `lower_entry`'s const-folder ate **`KOp::StorageLoad`** — its ONLY operand is the INDEX, so `sbuf.data[22]` looked like a fully-constant expression and the folder replaced the **MEMORY READ** with a literal. Every lowered shader reading a storage buffer at a fixed slot was miscompiled (the scene reads its light direction at word 22 and rendered BLACK); `BufferLoad`/`SharedLoad` escaped only BY ACCIDENT. **A memory read is NEVER a compile-time constant, however constant its index.** This was not one shader's bug: D3's variant matrix and content-hash dedup both run on the LOWERED graph, so a lowering pass that can silently zero a graph makes every cooked variant suspect. Pinned by the `[kir][lower][b7]` gate. ⛔ Preceded by a **MISREAD METRIC** that cost five wrong theories: the GEO-7 gate's `lit` is a COUNT of non-black pixels out of 256, not a colour channel — *before interpreting any number a gate prints, READ THE ASSERTION THAT PRODUCED IT*. **⭐⭐ 37.8 THE FRAME-LEVEL GRAPH (user question 2026-07-26: *"how different viewports will be rendered inside the main loop... mesh renderers in the file browser, animation players, vfx viewers, main viewport"*) — spec §14.** ⛔ `SceneRenderer::render()` CREATES AND EXECUTES ITS OWN GRAPH PER CALL, so an editor with a main viewport + an animation preview + 12 dirty thumbnails **SUBMITS 14 TIMES**, allocates every viewport's transients separately (peak VRAM = SUM not MAX), cannot order one viewport against another, and duplicates shared work (two views each rebuild the shadow atlas). **THE MODEL: ONE frame graph per FRAME; a viewport is a SUBGRAPH INSTANCE inside it** (`include` + `bind` + `as = "vp.main"` namespacing, 37.6) — so 12 thumbnails at 256^2 need ONE 256^2 depth buffer, the shadow atlas is declared ONCE outside any viewport and both readers order behind it automatically, and REN-8 timestamps become per-viewport for free. A thumbnail is **a different authored asset** (`thumbnail.frame.toml` — one pass, no shadows, no post), NEVER the main renderer with features switched off: cost tiering is authored, never `if (is_thumbnail)`. ⛔ **NO `render_thumbnail()`/`render_preview()` entry points** — a per-viewport-kind C++ function kills the top rule and is exactly how an editor look drifts from the shipped one. **⭐ 37.9 SCHEDULING (spec §15):** `ViewportPolicy{EveryFrame, OnDemand, Periodic}` + declared invalidation (`invalidate_on(asset/material/camera/resize)` — a hand-set dirty flag is a bug generator; a debug re-render + result-hash compare catches a MISSING dependency, because a stale thumbnail is indistinguishable from a correct one by eye) + a `ViewportBudget` in **measured GPU ms** fed by REN-8 timestamps. `EveryFrame` viewports are admitted FIRST (the main viewport is never starved by thumbnails); the rest go by priority **then AGE** — ⭐ ageing is what prevents permanent starvation of one thumbnail under a stream of high-priority invalidations; whatever does not fit stays dirty and is **REPORTED** (no silent caps). Settled steady state = **zero passes** for the whole browser. ⛔ **Readback becomes PER-VIEWPORT** (thumbnail capture pays the stall once; a live viewport never does) and the flag may remove only the WAIT, **never a barrier** — the REN-8 scar where readback-off silently dropped a layout transition the present path depended on. **GATE:** a user swaps `forward_csm` for `deferred_csm`, changes the cascade count, inserts a blur pass between two existing nodes, or authors a brand-new toon technique — **by editing ASSETS ONLY**, no engine recompile — and the sandbox renders it; plus the editor-shaped viewport gate (1 main + 16 on-demand thumbnails + 1 animation preview, budget 4/frame: all 16 render within 4 frames oldest-first, settled frames record thumbnail passes ZERO, invalidating one material re-renders exactly one, and it is **ONE submission per frame** throughout). |
| ⬜ | 141 | REN-38 | DEV | **⭐⭐⭐ THE FULL TECHNIQUE VOCABULARY — the renderer becomes INDEPENDENT OF ENGINE CODE.** **USER-DECLARED MUST (2026-07-26):** *"EVERY shader trick must be authorable"* · *"EVERYTHING MUST BE IN OUR TECHNIQUE VOCABULARY JUST LIKE CKIR! BUT THIS TIME WE ARE BUILDING ONTO CKIR! SO OUR RENDERER WILL BE FULLY INDEPENDENT OF ENGINE CODE"*. **THE GAP, MEASURED BY READING THE CODE: 31 device draw verbs + 14 CKIR shader STAGES + ~30 CKIR shading LIBRARIES, reachable through 4 working pass kinds and 3 techniques.** ⛔ **THE RULE FOR EVERY ROW BELOW: each gap is a MISSING VOCABULARY ENTRY, never a missing special case** — if a technique needs something composition cannot express, extend the VOCABULARY, never the renderer. ⛔⛔ **TWO THINGS PREVIOUSLY MARKED DONE THAT WERE NOT:** (1) REN-37.5 persistent/ping-pong is DEVICE-COMPLETE AND GATED BUT NOT AUTHORABLE (`FrameResourceKind` is still `{TransientImage, TransientBuffer}`); (2) REN-37 authored the TECHNIQUE axis and left the MATERIAL axis a C++ FUNCTION POINTER (`MaterialTemplate::build_surface`) — there is no `.crdm`. **Each item below is its own ROW with its own ID, so nothing hides inside prose.** |
| | 141 | **38-A** | | ── **THE PASS-KIND VOCABULARY** — what an asset can ask the device to DO ── | |
| ✅ | 141 | 38-A0 | DEV | **THE RECORDING-PATH AUDIT (the true prerequisite)** — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-26, BOTH BACKENDS.** ⛔⛔⛔ **A DRAW VERB WITHOUT A FRAME-RECORDING PATH FALLS THROUGH TO THE SYNCHRONOUS PATH AND TRIES TO SUBMIT WHILE THE FRAME COMMAND BUFFER IS ALREADY RECORDING — IT SEGFAULTS.** Found while gating 38-A3; invisible from the pass-kind audit because pass kinds sit one layer above it. **⭐ ROOT CAUSE, and it is the SHARED SHAPE of nearly every unrecordable verb: their synchronous bodies call `vkResetDescriptorPool` / rebuild the descriptor heap on the GLOBAL pool**, which invalidates every descriptor set already bound by earlier passes in the same command buffer. They OWN the pool, so they cannot be guests in someone else’s frame. Every port below is therefore the same two-part change: allocate from the FRAME pool, and never touch global state. **✅ VULKAN RECORDS (12 of 25):** `draw_storage` · `draw_storage_depth{,_load}` · `draw_storage_depth_only{,_load}` · `draw_storage_shadowed_depth{,_load}` · `draw_storage_textured_depth{,_load}` · `draw_textured`/`draw_shadow` (via `record_textured`) · `draw_overlay` · **`draw_bindless` (NEW, 38-A1a)**. **✅ DX12 RECORDS (9 of 25):** `draw_storage` · `draw_storage_depth{,_load}` · `draw_storage_depth_only{,_load}` · `draw_storage_sampled_depth` · `draw_storage_shadowed_depth_load` · `draw_storage_textured_depth_load` · `draw_sampled`. ⛔⛔ **CROSS-BACKEND ASYMMETRY FOUND — AND IT BREAKS "ONE ASSET, BOTH BACKENDS": `draw_overlay` RECORDS ON VULKAN AND DOES NOT EXIST ON DX12 AT ALL.** The sandbox’s grid/gizmo overlay pass therefore composes into the frame on Vulkan and cannot on DX12. **Every 38-A1x row below must land on BOTH backends, and parity is part of each row’s gate — a verb recordable on one backend only is a worse state than one recordable on neither, because it renders correctly right up until someone switches API.** **DOES NOT RECORD (the 38-A1x rows):** `draw` · `draw_depth` · `draw_bindless_depth` · `draw_conservative` · `draw_gbuffer` · `draw_mesh{,_indirect,_vrs,_bindless_depth}` · `draw_tess` · `draw_visbuffer` · `draw_vrs` · `draw_wboit` · (+ `draw_overlay`, `draw_bindless`, `draw_textured`, `draw_shadow` on DX12). |
| ✅ | 141 | 38-A1a | DEV | **RECORD: bindless (draw_bindless)** — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-26, BOTH BACKENDS, PARITY GATED.** ⛔⛔ **THE ROOT CAUSE, and it is the shape every remaining 38-A1x row will hit:** the synchronous body OWNS the descriptor pool. Vulkan called `vkResetDescriptorPool` on the GLOBAL pool; DX12 minted its SRV array into the GLOBAL heap at FIXED slots AND reset the command allocator + list. Inside a frame each of those invalidates or stomps descriptors earlier passes are still using — which is why the verb SEGFAULTED the moment an authored pass called it. **The fix is the same two-part change every port needs: allocate from the FRAME pool/ring, and touch nothing global.** VK `record_bindless` + `frame_alloc_bindless_set`; DX12 `record_bindless` + `frame_alloc_bindless_run` — ⛔ CONTIGUOUS on DX12, because a root descriptor TABLE addresses N consecutive slots from ONE GPU handle, so per-slot allocation would interleave with other passes and the array would read whatever landed between them. ⛔ **A THIRD FAILURE, in the GATE rather than the engine, worth keeping:** the test shader declared the bindless array at binding 1 (the SINGLE sampled image) instead of binding 3, so the pipeline accessed a descriptor nothing ever wrote — `VUID-vkCmdDraw-None-08114`. The array is a FIXED-SIZE `kBindlessMax` declaration, so a shorter one is a DIFFERENT TYPE, not a subset. **GATE (both backends, identical claims):** two offscreen passes write a RED and a GREEN target; a third declares BOTH as reads and composites tex[0].r into RED and tex[1].g into GREEN — ⭐ a mapping chosen so the two failure modes are DISTINGUISHABLE (binding only the first read leaves green 0; binding them SWAPPED leaves red 0), where "not black" would have passed under both. VK 13/1 · DX12 13/1 · no regressions (VK frame-graph 238/16, DX12 1193/119). **This also un-blocked 38-A3, whose gate now runs for real.** |
| ✅ | 141 | 38-A1b | DEV | **RECORD: MRT (draw_gbuffer)** — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-26, BOTH BACKENDS — gated end to end by 38-A4.** ⭐ **DESIGN DECISION, and it is not a shortcut:** the graph CANNOT use `draw_gbuffer`, because `IGBufferTarget` is a self-contained resource with its own images, readbacks and lifetime — handing one to the graph would put a SECOND, UNTRACKED allocator inside it and defeat the transient aliasing the graph exists for. So a NEW verb `draw_storage_mrt` takes N of the graph’s OWN targets, which is exactly what the asset already writes: `writes = ["albedo", "normal", "material"]`. `draw_gbuffer` remains the standalone path; neither is a special case of the other. Appended at the vtable END (D135) with a NO-OP default, so a backend without it fails VISIBLY rather than silently drawing into attachment 0 only. **VK:** N `VkRenderingAttachmentInfo` from N target views + an inline depth attachment (a real G-buffer pass is depth-tested); ⛔ it must NOT insert its own layout transitions the way the synchronous `draw_gbuffer` does — the graph has already moved every declared write to COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL, and a redundant UNDEFINED-source transition would DISCARD the contents the graph is tracking. **DX12:** each graph transient owns its OWN single-RTV heap (the REN-3.2 rule — a target renders to its heap’s START handle, so a shared heap would stack every attachment on slot 0); `OMSetRenderTargets` accepts an ARRAY of CPU handles from DIFFERENT heaps when `RTsSingleHandleToDescriptorRange` is FALSE, which is exactly the shape the graph produces — no descriptor copying needed. **EXECUTOR:** `PassRec` now collects ALL write handles (`writes_all[]`), mirroring what 38-A3 did for reads; ⛔ a write that does not resolve ABORTS the pass rather than shifting every later attachment down a slot — the shader writes `SV_Target1` and it would land in attachment 0’s image. `RasterMrt` split out of the shared single-target geometry path it used to borrow. No regressions: VK frame-graph 238/16, DX12 frame-graph 190/12. |
| ✅ | 141 | 38-A1c | DEV | **RECORD: mesh + task (draw_mesh, _vrs, _indirect, _bindless_depth)** — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-26, BOTH BACKENDS (all four verbs).** `record_mesh` covers all four through one body with optional parameters, because they differ only in what they add. ⛔ **THREE THINGS THAT MUST BE RIGHT OR THE BIND IS INVALID:** (1) `set_draw_state(..., mesh_draw = true)` — a mesh pipeline has NO vertex-input state and leaving that dynamic state set makes the bind invalid; (2) the VERTEX stage must be EXPLICITLY UNBOUND (bind `VK_NULL_HANDLE` to it) or the driver still expects a vertex pipeline beside the mesh one; (3) TASK is bound ONLY when the program has one — a mesh-only program declares `NO_TASK_SHADER`, and binding a null task stage is NOT the same as not binding it. `group_count` is TASK workgroups when `has_task()` (they amplify), MESH workgroups otherwise. **_bindless_depth** reuses 38-A1a’s `frame_alloc_bindless_set` + a depth attachment; **_indirect** takes the workgroup count from a device buffer (`draw_mesh_tasks_indirect`) so the CPU never learns it — the GPU-driven half. ⛔ **A BUG I INTRODUCED AND CAUGHT:** the first `_vrs` attempt called `record_mesh` with count 0 and dispatched afterwards — but `record_mesh` CLOSES the rendering scope, so the dispatch would have landed outside it. The rate is therefore a PARAMETER of `record_mesh`, applied after `set_draw_state`’s 1x1 default and before the dispatch, which is the only place it can legally go. **DX12:** one `record_mesh` for all four too — ⛔ mesh shaders emit their OWN topology so there is NO `IASetPrimitiveTopology` (issuing one is invalid for a mesh PSO), and the amplification stage is part of the PSO, so unlike Vulkan there is nothing to bind separately. `_indirect` uses `ExecuteIndirect` with the DISPATCH_MESH signature; ⛔ the args buffer must already be in INDIRECT_ARGUMENT state, which inside a frame is the GRAPH’s job (it is a declared read), so the synchronous path’s transition pair is absent. `_bindless_depth` reuses 38-A1a’s frame descriptor RUN. VK 3634/211 · DX12 1193/119. |
| ✅ | 141 | 38-A1d | DEV | **RECORD: tessellation (draw_tess)** — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-26, BOTH BACKENDS.** The portable displacement path (HW without mesh shaders). Same attachment-and-draw shape as 38-A1h’s `record_plain`, plus the only two things tessellation adds: PATCH-LIST topology and the patch control-point count. VK `record_tess` binds all FOUR stages (VS -> TCS -> TES -> FS) — the one raster verb here that does; DX12 generalises `record_plain` into `record_plain_topology` (`D3D_PRIMITIVE_TOPOLOGY_4_CONTROL_POINT_PATCHLIST`), since once the graph owns the transitions the topology is the ONLY difference from a plain draw. No allocator/list reset, no transitions, no MSAA resolve, no readback — all graph-owned. Full suites green: VK 3634/211 · DX12 1193/119. |
| ✅ | 141 | 38-A1e | DEV | **RECORD: visibility buffer (draw_visbuffer)** — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-26, BOTH BACKENDS.** `record_plain` with ONE difference that matters: an R32_UINT target clears with a **UINT** clear value, not a float one. ⛔ Writing the id through `clearValue.color.float32[0]` would reinterpret its BIT PATTERN as a float — id 1 becomes 1.4e-45 — and every pixel would read "background" while the draw itself looked perfectly correct. VK `record_visbuffer` sets `color.uint32[0]`; DX12 reuses `record_plain_topology` (its `ClearRenderTargetView` takes a float[4] even for R32_UINT and D3D12 reinterprets the bits, matching the synchronous path). No allocator/list reset, no transitions, no readback — graph-owned. VK 275/20 (frame-graph + visbuffer + ren38) · DX12 1193/119. |
| ✅ | 141 | 38-A1f | DEV | **RECORD: OIT (draw_wboit)** — ⛔ **RESOLVED BY ARCHITECTURE, NOT BY A PORT — and this row changes shape as a result.** `draw_wboit` ALLOCATES ITS OWN accum + revealage images inside the verb and destroys them at the end. | ✅ | 141 | 38-B1 | DEV | **persistent_image / pingpong_image IN THE ASSET** — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-26.** The DEVICE half landed in REN-37.5; the asset could not NAME one until now. ⛔ A transient’s memory is aliased and retired the instant its last reader finishes — right for a G-buffer, fatal for anything TEMPORAL, so TAA history, SSR/DDGI/ReSTIR reuse, auto-exposure and the REN-37.9 cached thumbnail had no resource to live in. ⭐⭐ **PING-PONG NEEDS NO NEW SYNTAX, AND THAT IS THE DESIGN:** a **READ** of a `pingpong_image` resolves to the PREVIOUS frame’s image and a **WRITE** to THIS frame’s, with the pair rotating each frame. That IS what "history buffer" means — and because the author never holds the parity bit, they cannot hold it wrong; a `$prev`/`$curr` spelling would have handed them the classic one-frame-stale bug to make. Implemented as two persistent images under two keys. ⛔ **THE KEY IS A HASH OF THE NAME, NOT THE DECLARATION INDEX** — an index shifts the moment someone adds a resource above it in the file, and a persistent image is looked up BY KEY across frames, so a shifted key silently swaps two histories (TAA reading the auto-exposure buffer) or discards one. **COOK-TIME:** an ABSOLUTE size is required (`PersistentNeedsSize`) — a scale-relative extent changes the instant the output resizes, which recreates the image and DISCARDS the history silently mid-session; and a ping-pong resource must be both read AND written (`PingPongNeedsBothWays`), since a one-way one never rotates and would read the same stale contents forever. A persistent image is EXEMPT from "every resource must be written", because being read for many frames and rewritten only on change is exactly the steady state this row exists for. ⭐ **GATE:** the pass READS and WRITES the same authored name in ONE frame — legal only because the two resolve to DIFFERENT images; were they one handle the graph would see a read-after-write on a single node and reject it as a CYCLE, so building and executing twice IS the proof the pair rotated. Running the suites on Mesa llvmpipe (a CPU Vulkan driver: subgroup width 8, no `robustBufferAccess` forgiveness) turned 39 red tests into a list of REAL defects the NVIDIA-only history had absorbed. ⛔⛔ **THREE OOB CLASSES, all silent before:** (1) the sort's digit-match complements a u32 ballot, so `~bal` SETS the phantom lanes above an 8-wide subgroup — ghost counts, staged ranks past the shared arrays (fix: start from the ACTIVE-lane mask); (2) UNGUARDED TAIL THREADS everywhere a dispatch rounds up (2DGS project, the whole 3DGS family, both RT oracles — fix: a declared `count` + an If-guard; ⚠ the scatter kernel's grid is `count x max_cover`, so the bound is the PRODUCT); (3) EAGER-`Select` SENTINEL LOADS — a GPU evaluates both arms exactly as the scalar oracle does, so StopThePop's not-found index and the path tracer's light index both loaded out of bounds regardless of the discard (the path tracer's clamped only its UPPER bound, so every wall UNDERFLOWED a u32 subtract to ~4e9). **The systemic fix that keeps the class dead: `eval_cpu_kernel` now ASSERTS on OOB instead of imitating robustness with 0.0** — which is precisely how (2) and (3) passed every oracle gate for months. ⛔⛔ **AND THE LAST FAILURE WAS A NAME-MANGLING BUG WEARING A GPU COSTUME:** `component_id_by_name` matched `typeid(T).name()` with only the MSVC decoration, so on the Itanium ABI (`N3crd5scene12MeshRendererE` — length-prefixed, trailing `E`) it NEVER matched; every authored component filter rejected every group, the draw list resolved EMPTY, and SceneRenderer frames were black on every gcc build since REN-36.3-b. Also fixed: `taskShader` was never ENABLED while task shader objects were created (illegal everywhere; NVIDIA tolerated it), the shader-object TASK-bind completeness rule, DXIL PSIn register packing (FS inputs must declare SORTED BY LOCATION — this had broken EVERY DX12 scene gate behind a `SKIP`), the AS→MS payload contract, spec-derived precision envelopes replacing NVIDIA-calibrated ones, environment-keyed autotune rows (the same sm_89 under WSL measured the win32-tuned tile 2.27x slower than its own winner), device-typed perf floors, pinned Vulkan headers on Linux, and nine gcc-only portability defects. ⭐⭐ **THE DOCTRINE:** a second implementation is not a nice-to-have — it is the only thing that can see a subgroup-width assumption or a silent OOB, and **when a "GPU bug" splits by COMPILER rather than by DEVICE, stop looking at the GPU.** FINAL: Linux gcc-release 5221/5221 · Windows win-debug 5482/5482 · all touched files tidy-clean. |

The corrupt submission REMOVED THE DEVICE, after which every `CreateCommittedResource` in the process failed (a 480-byte staging buffer "unavailable" on a healthy adapter — the symptom that exposed it). The overlay is the first caller that ever uploads from inside a pass, so nothing had exercised it; Vulkan is immune by construction (its sync path opens its own command buffer). Uploads during recording now join the FRAME's list with the same UAV↔COPY_DEST bracket, staging retained per ring slot until that slot's fence retires. **RESULT: the gizmo, its cones, the grid and the wire/solid shapes all render on DX12** (screenshot-verified). **🟡 OPEN — the DX12 scene renders correct GEOMETRY but BLACK SHADING.** Established by measurement, not theory: the VS and FS are SEMANTICALLY IDENTICAL across backends (canonicalised statement diff of the same cooked graph through both emitters: 283/283 VS and 680/680 FS statements, every difference a legitimate language spelling — mat4+transpose, texture/Sample, `*`/`mul`, SampleCmp); the VSOut/PSIn interfaces match exactly (float3/float4/float4/float2, SV_Position last, PSIn sorted); and it is NOT shadows (black with the shadows-off tier too), NOT the asset root (black on the embedded pack), NOT the technique (`unlit` — which needs only the tint varying — is black too), NOT geometry (silhouettes are pixel-correct). ⛔ Two of my own measurements in this hunt were WRONG and cost time: a "non-black pixel" count that was really counting the CLEAR COLOUR, and isolating with `forward_basic` — a frame with NO display transform, so it renders dark on BOTH backends and proves nothing. **⭐⭐ ROOT CAUSE FOUND (user's instinct — "normals off, or the depth is reversed" — was the depth half): the DX12 frame graph IGNORES `depth_buffer = true` ON A COLOUR TRANSIENT. `grep -c 'depth_buffer\|companion'` = 8+ sites in `vulkan_raster_context.cpp` (the 38-G1 companion-depth image, its memory, its barriers) and ZERO in `dx12_raster_context.cpp`.** So `scene_hdr` has no depth on DX12, `has_depth()` is false, the forward pass binds NO DSV, depth testing never happens, and whichever triangle rasterizes last wins — which is exactly what the pixels show: silhouettes correct, INTERIORS visible (we see inside the teal box and the icosahedron), and blackness correlated with face orientation because the surviving surfaces are back-facing and their normals point away from the light. **The decisive experiment: a frame asset flipped to standard-Z (`clear_depth = 1.0`, `depth = "LessEqual"`) BROKE Vulkan exactly as predicted and left DX12 PIXEL-IDENTICAL — a backend that does not change when the declared depth state changes is not testing depth at all.** ⛔⛔ TWO INSTRUMENT FAILURES cost most of this hunt and both are now scars: (1) a "non-black pixel" count that was really counting the CLEAR COLOUR, and isolating with `forward_basic` — a frame with NO display transform, so it renders dark on BOTH backends and proves nothing; (2) **every technique-based bisection was VOID because with shadows active the renderer draws with its SHADOWED program (cooked from `forward_csm`) regardless of the technique the frame asset declares** — a CONSTANT-RED control returned zero red pixels on BOTH backends and exposed it; after `--no-shadows` the same control returned 151804 identical pixels on both. ⭐ The rule that saved it: a probe that modifies a shader is worthless until a control with a stated-in-advance expected value passes. **THE FIX (next):** create the companion depth resource + DSV for a graph-owned colour transient declaring `depth_buffer`, wire `has_depth()/dsv()/dsv_format()/clear_flags()` on it, and give it the transition/lifetime walk its Vulkan twin has. Also still open and separate: `--pull-draws` on DX12 renders NOTHING (the non-indexed multi-draw path). **⭐⭐ RENDERDOC PASS ON THE RESIDUAL SHADOW DELTA (23628 px after the companion-depth fix; user's read: "the depth part is offsetted, objects self-shadowing"): the ENTIRE DX12 shadow PIPELINE STATE IS CORRECT — captured a D3D12 frame (`renderdoccmd capture` + F12, converted with `renderdoccmd convert -c xml` and queried programmatically, since the winget build ships no Python module).** Ground truth from the capture: depth clears = atlas slices 0..3 → 1.0 and the companion depth → 0.0 (all `CLEAR_FLAG_DEPTH`); DepthFunc = `LESS_EQUAL` ×4 (cascades) + `GREATER_EQUAL` ×2 (forward) + one depth-disabled (post); the shadow comparison sampler = `LESS_EQUAL`; viewports = 2048×2048 for all four cascade passes and 1280×720 for forward/post, all `MinDepth 0 .. MaxDepth 1`; rasterizer = cull NONE, front-CCW, **DepthBias 0 / SlopeScaled 0 / Clamp 0** (identical to Vulkan — so there is NO API-level depth offset, and the self-shadowing cannot be a bias-state divergence); the atlas resource = `R32_TYPELESS 2048×2048 array=4` with its SRV `R32_FLOAT TEXTURE2DARRAY arraySize=4 firstSlice=0`; the companion depth = `D32_FLOAT 1280×720` (present, i.e. the D2 fix is live in the capture). **So the residual is a SHADER-COMPUTED VALUE, not a binding or a state** — and the matrix path is not it either: `MatFromCols` emits `transpose(float4x4(cols))` on HLSL vs `mat4(cols)` on GLSL and `MatVecMul` emits `mul(M,v)` vs `M*v`, which are the SAME M·v with columns c0..c3. **Next: output the shadow VISIBILITY term as colour (constant-red control FIRST — see the probe-control scar), then `sz` and the sampled depth, on both backends.** **⭐⭐⭐ CLOSED — ROOT CAUSE: THE CLIP-SPACE Y CONVENTION.** Vulkan's NDC has +Y pointing DOWN the framebuffer; D3D12's points UP, and the engine sets a POSITIVE-height `VkViewport` (Vulkan's native convention). So **every render target is stored vertically MIRRORED between the backends.** That is invisible for ordinary rendering — a colour target and the fullscreen pass that consumes it flip TOGETHER, which is exactly why the beauty frames matched to 304 px and why 'is it flipped?' kept answering NO. It becomes visible the instant a shader turns a CLIP position back into a TEXTURE coordinate: `uv = ndc*0.5 + 0.5` bakes one convention in, so the other backend reads the MIRRORED ROW. Shadow mapping is precisely that operation, and the atlas has no fullscreen pass to flip it back. **Nothing in the shadow system was wrong**, which is why the audit cleared, in order: the cascade fit and cull (CPU plane test vs shader containment agreed — c2 818/723, c3 3306/3273), world position and `sz`, every pipeline state via RenderDoc, the per-slice DSVs (`FirstArraySlice=l, ArraySize=1`, own heap each), `image_layer`, both emitters' array-layer handling, `MatFromCols`+`MatVecMul` composition, `storage_read_only`/t0-vs-u0, the variant hash (`cascade` IS hashed), the indirect-args ring (bumped 32→512: no change), pass-state leakage, transient lifetime and the barriers. **The turning point was `--pull-draws` being BIT-IDENTICAL on both backends** — that proved the defect sat in neither draw path, so it had to be a convention, not a state. **The one-line proof:** flipping V in the technique made DX12 match Vulkan at **0.83%** (from **12.94%**). **THE FIX — the convention belongs to the BACKEND and is folded in by the ENGINE, never by a technique:** (1) `IRasterContext::ndc_y_points_down()` appended at the vtable END (Vulkan true, DX12 false); (2) `SceneShaderConfig::flip_clip_y`, stamped in `cook_fs` — the ONE place every scene FS is cooked, so no call site can forget it; (3) the `csm_light_vp` binding resolver NEGATES THE MATRIX'S Y ROW when set, which flips `lp.y` for the FS ONLY — the shadow VS keeps reading the raw header matrix, so what is RASTERIZED is untouched and only the LOOKUP is corrected. ⛔ Flipping the shared matrix instead would flip write AND read = no net change. The graph changes, so the content hash separates the two cooks automatically, and an authored technique keeps ONE portable formula. **RESULT: DX12 12.94% → 0.83% differing; residual 2809 px at median magnitude 1, p99 = 2 (AA/rounding); Vulkan BIT-IDENTICAL (0 px changed); 125/125 gates green across BOTH backends; tidy clean.** ⚠ Every future clip-derived UV has the same hazard (SSR, TAA reprojection, planar reflections, DDGI) — use the declared backend fact. ⚠ Still open and separate: the Vulkan context never sets depth-clamp/clip state at all (no `vkCmdSetDepthClampEnableEXT`) while DX12 hardcodes `DepthClipEnable = TRUE`; under VK_EXT_shader_object an unset dynamic state is undefined. |

HONEST: the reduce margin is inherently small (memory-bound streams DRAM; the value is op-generality, not the ratio). **FLASH-ATTENTION FUSION LANDED (the marquee AS-4 crush): a first-class `KOp::Attention` intrinsic (O = softmax(Q·Kᵀ·scale)·V) that the CUDA backend FUSES into ONE tiled online-softmax (flash) kernel** (`emit_attention_flash_cuda` + `select_attention_tile` in `ckir_cuda.hpp`; the CPU oracle `eval_cpu` computes the naive reference; a FAST tier — online softmax reassociates ⇒ ULP-tolerant). The S×S scores NEVER touch DRAM — the structural moat the unfused 3-kernel path (which round-trips S² through DRAM) cannot cross. **Gate GREEN: intrinsic oracle == expanded/module attention (GM-6) to 1e-12; the fused flash kernel via `cu.run` == the naive oracle to 3e-9 (S=256/512/300, the last exercises the S-remainder guards); the crush (`bench/gpu-compute/flash_attention_bench.cu`, `docs/bench/2026-07-23-as4-flash-attention-fusion.md`) GROWS with S — flash vs unfused 2.02× @ S=2048, 3.93× @ S=4096 (D=64, both ~1e-8 vs a double CPU ref); at small S flash loses (occupancy-starved) ⇒ the fused/tile choice is schedulable. CUDA suite 81404/18, tidy-clean.** **FLASH AUTOTUNER LANDED: the (BR,BC) tile is now SCHEDULED — `enumerate_attention_schedules` (backend-free + unit-tested, 16 tiles D=64, 64×32 a member) + `KirBackendCuda::time_attention` (GPU-event-timed) drive the same enumerate→measure→oracle-validate→pick-best loop; the search finds BR=128×BC=32 beats the hard-coded BR=64 across S∈{512,1024,2048,4096}, all 16 tiles oracle-correct. **AS-2-for-attention WIRED: the measured winners are CHECKED IN (`ckir_attention_db.inc`, `{device,S,D,BR,BC}`) + `lookup_attention_tuned` replays them — `select_attention_tile(dim,S,device,…)` consults the DB first (per-device exact-(S,D)), heuristic 64×32 on a miss (the `select_schedule`/`lookup_tuned` pattern), so `run()` emits the TUNED flash kernel with no runtime search; the flash-vs-oracle test (S=512, now DB-tuned 128×32) still matches to 3.1e-9. CUDA suite 81496/19, kir [autotune] 257/9, tidy-clean.** **CONV-via-FFT: ALREADY SHIPPED (`build_fft{1,2}d_convolution*`, board `2026-07-13-gpu-fft-cufft-gold.md`) — crushes cuFFT in 4/5 regimes (1-D 1.99×, 2-D 256² 2.90×, batched 1.16×, R2C 1.14×); the one loss (2-D single-image 1024² 0.40×) is MEMORY-bound (4 transpose passes = 88 MB vs cuFFT 56 MB), NOT the FMA wall. ⛔ 2026-07-23 verified: a fast/ULP compute tier (drop `precise` ⇒ driver FMA) gives **0% on the conv** (memory-bound) — FMA is the WRONG lever (the real one is transpose-on-write fusion, a fresh-context-scale FFT rewrite) — experiment REVERTED. Attention DB expanded to D∈{32,64} (D=128 spills, other GPUs env-blocked). **NEXT: transpose-on-write FFT fusion (the named conv lever); more devices/D into DBs when hardware available.** |
| ✅ | 59 | AS-5 | IR | **equality-saturation superoptimization** — `KGraph::superoptimize()`: canonicalizes commutative ops (Add/Mul → smaller-operand-first) then folds/CSEs/DCEs to a FIXPOINT (rewrite-until-stable = equality saturation), so commutatively-equal subexpressions collapse — the CSE greedy `optimize()` misses (its `node_equal` matches operands POSITIONALLY, so `a*b`≠`b*a`). ⭐ KEY INSIGHT: for a BIT-EXACT compiler this is the WHOLE safe superopt space — reassociation/distributivity change float rounding, so the aggressive structural rewrites (Flash-Attention-class fusion) belong to the fast/ULP tier (a separate, non-bit-exact concern), NOT here. **Gate GREEN (kir): on `(a*b+c)*(b*a+c)` greedy optimize → 9 nodes, equality-saturation → 7 nodes, BIT-IDENTICAL result; [opt] unaffected; tidy-clean.** |
| 🔄 | 60 | AS-6 | DEV | **cross-backend / cross-device autotuning** — **AS-6a DONE: the tuning DB is DEVICE-KEYED.** Each `TuningEntry` carries the GPU arch (`"sm_89"`); `lookup_tuned(op, device, m, n, k)` + `select_schedule(g, node, device)` replay per-GPU (a different device MISSES → heuristic fallback, since the best tile schedule genuinely differs per device); nullptr = wildcard. The CUDA backend passes `impl.arch`; the CLI writes the device tag (`kir_autotune.exe` → device-tagged rows). **Gate GREEN: sm_89 hits, sm_75/sm_120 miss, wildcard hits (37 assertions); CUDA 81389/17 no regression; tidy-clean.** **AS-6b DONE: the autotuner drives VULKAN/SPIR-V.** `emit_contract_tiled_glsl_sched` is the PARAMETERIZED GLSL GEMM (block BT×BT, K-depth BK, TM×TM register microtile, plain-float ⇒ shaderc fuses FMA), the Vulkan analogue of `emit_contract_tiled_cuda`. The AS-6b test enumerates (BT,BK,TM), compiles GLSL→SPIR-V, dispatches + `last_gpu_ms`-times each, oracle-validates, keeps the fastest. **Gate GREEN (Vulkan): 512³ autotuned BT64·BK16·TM4 → 6341 GFLOP/s, 7/7 GLSL schedules oracle-correct — and the Vulkan winner DIFFERS from CUDA's 128×128×8, proving per-backend tuning (⇒ why AS-6a device-keys). tidy-clean.** **AS-6 COMPLETE: the auto-scheduler is a CROSS-BACKEND + CROSS-DEVICE compiler property — the same enumerate→measure→oracle-validate→cache framework drives CUDA (PTX) AND Vulkan (SPIR-V), each device replaying its own tuned schedule.** |
| — | — | — | — | **══════ GM band — GENERICS + MODULES (the authoring language layer): CKIR from "build graphs in raw C++" → a composable, type-polymorphic surface (Slang-class). The 2nd world-class gap after the auto-scheduler; the foundation for a shared kernel stdlib + the node editor's node types. User-directed 2026-07-22. ══════** |
| ✅ | 61 | GM-1 | IR | **KModule + generic KFn + monomorphizing call** (`ckir_module.hpp`) — a MODULE is a registry of named GENERIC graph functions; a `KFn` is a reusable subgraph builder GENERIC over element dtype (bound from the args = monomorphization) + shape (free — ops infer it). `KModule::call(g, name, args)` INSTANTIATES a function by name, TYPE-CHECKED at the boundary (arity + uniform dtype ⇒ -1 on mismatch, never a wrong graph). A first `stdlib` module (silu/softplus/gelu + **`linear` = a 3-arg generic GEMM+bias**). **Gate GREEN (kir): silu/gelu instantiate per dtype(F32/F64)+shape({4}/{8}/{3,5}), boundary type-checks (arity+unknown-name → -1); fns COMPOSE (softplus∘silu, dups CSE'd 18→11 via superoptimize); a REAL MLP LAYER `gelu(linear(x,W,b))` authored ENTIRELY from named module fns is oracle-correct (its GEMM is autotuner-scheduled ⇒ inherits the vendor-crushing schedule) — 31 assertions, tidy-clean.** ⛔ scar: CKIR's 1-D `broadcast([N],[M,N])` aligns the FIRST axis (per-ROW) — a linear/column bias needs a `reshape([N]→[1,N])` first ([[feedback_ckir_1d_broadcast_aligns_first_axis_bias_needs_reshape]]). |
| ✅ | 62 | GM-3 | IR | **first-class call-node in the IR** (the serialization + node-editor seam) — `KOp::Call` (appended at enum END, cook-stable): a graph node that HOLDS a named module call — the function id in `iidx`, args in the ext pool, output shape from the function's **shape rule** (`KFnShapeRule` on `KFn`; `linear_shape` gives [M,K]·[K,N]→[M,N], activations = shape-preserving) computed WITHOUT building the body. `KModule::call_node` builds one (same arity+uniform-dtype boundary checks as `call`); `KModule::lower_calls(g, roots)` INLINES each Call into its `body` and `KGraph::redirect(from,to)` splices the inlined output in place — Calls resolve in id order (a Call consuming a Call, `gelu(linear)`, works), roots updated, then optimize()/superoptimize() DCEs the dead Call nodes. The emitters + oracle NEVER see a Call (lowered away first); `optimize()` treats a stray Call as opaque (never folds it). This is what makes a module-authored graph SERIALIZABLE as named calls (not a flattened blob) — the node-editor's node type + the separate-compilation seam. **Gate GREEN (kir): `gelu(linear(x,W,b))` authored via call_node → 2 real `KOp::Call` nodes shaped by rule (linear→[2,2] without a body), `lower_calls` inlines both + optimizes to the directly-`call`-inlined graph BIT-FOR-BIT (`bit_equal`), oracle-correct — 45 [module] assertions (4 cases), tidy-clean.** |
| ✅ | 63 | GM-4 | IR | **generics over INT/UINT element types** (dtype polymorphism beyond float) — a dtype-generic numeric group (`register_numerics`): `muladd(a,b,c)=a·b+c`, `clamp(x,lo,hi)`. The SAME generic body monomorphizes to a correct subgraph for F64/F32/I32/U32 — and on a 32-bit integer type the Mul/Add WRAP mod 2^32 exactly as every GPU backend does (the oracle's `apply_binary_typed` wrap path, [[feedback_ckir_oracle_u32_arithmetic_must_wrap_mod32]]). Kept SEPARATE from `register_activations` (float-only transcendentals) so a consumer picks the group + the activations' count stays stable. **Gate GREEN (kir): `muladd` monomorphizes F64/F32 (3·4+5=17 exact), U32 (100000²+7 WRAPS to 1410065415), I32 (50000² signed-wraps NEGATIVE) — graph structure IDENTICAL across dtypes (monomorphization = pure dtype substitution); `clamp` int-generic; the GM-3 call-node carries the U32 monomorphization through lowering wrap-correct — 74 [module] assertions (5 cases), tidy-clean.** |
| ✅ | 64 | GM-5 | IR | **SEPARATE COMPILATION / intra-module LINKAGE** — a module function COMPOSED from OTHER module functions resolved by NAME (late binding via a module handle in `KFn::user`), NOT hand-inlined: `ffn(x,W,b)=gelu∘linear` is authored as `m->call("linear")` then `m->call("gelu")`, so the callees are independently compilable and a consumer names `ffn` blind to its internals. `register_stdlib` wires the whole library (activations + numerics + the composite `ffn` capturing `&m`). Combined with GM-3 this is the full authoring→serialize→link story: a graph can hold `ffn` as ONE named call-node, and `lower_calls` EXPANDS it through the linkage into the full linear+gelu subgraph. **Gate GREEN (kir): `ffn` links linear+gelu (7 fns registered), equals the hand-composed `gelu(linear(x,W,b))` graph BIT-FOR-BIT; as a call-node it is ONE serializable named call that lowers/expands through the linkage to 0 remaining Calls, oracle-correct — 85 [module] assertions (6 cases), full kir binary GREEN (all cases), tidy-clean.** |
| ✅ | 65 | GM-6 | IR | **PRODUCTION NEURAL BLOCKS authored from named module functions** (user-directed 2026-07-23: "deepen GM stdlib") — `register_neural`: `layernorm(x,γ,β)` (mean/var over the last axis + per-column γ·norm+β), `softmax(x)` (stable exp/Σexp), `attention(q,k,v)` = softmax(q·kᵀ/√d)·v (which LINKS `softmax` by name, GM-5), and the payoff `transformer_block` = pre-norm self-attention + FFN with TWO residuals, a 9-arg composite that LINKS layernorm/attention/linear/gelu. ⭐ KEY: NO new KOps — every block is pure COMPOSITION of existing CKIR ops (reduce/broadcast/exp/sqrt/rsqrt/contract/permute), so all FIVE backends already emit them and the CPU oracle already certifies them; γ/β use the `[N]→[1,N]` per-column reshape ([[feedback_ckir_1d_broadcast_aligns_first_axis_bias_needs_reshape]]). **Gate GREEN (kir): layernorm/softmax/attention verified vs HAND-COMPUTED math (the primitives are correct, not just self-consistent — softmax rows sum to 1, attention S=2·D=2 matches by hand); the 9-arg `transformer_block` (11 fns registered) is right-shaped [S,N]→[S,N] and matches the hand-composed sequence BIT-FOR-BIT — 115 [module] assertions (7 cases), full kir binary 52575/243, tidy-clean.** **GM COMPLETE for the pre-node-editor scope: CKIR is a composable, type-polymorphic, dtype-generic authoring language with named + serializable + linkable functions that expresses PRODUCTION neural nets (a real transformer block) — Slang-class. Node-editor FRONTEND deferred until after crd-hesap (per user).** |

**Pairings to remember** (why the order interleaves IR + DEV): raster **B3-c/d/e ↔ C1(done)/C4** · ray tracing **B9 ↔ C3**
· neural **B10 ↔ C6** · work-graphs **B11 ↔ C5**. Each IR (shader) slice needs its DEV (device) half to be *exercisable*,
so they land adjacent.

**One caveat on the done rows:** B0's dedicated multi-config DoD (`-IncludeRelease` + clang-cl + gcc for `crd-kir`) was
folded into the D-008-close full sweep (win-debug/asan/shipping/tidy all green 2026-07-11); the gcc leg stays env-blocked
(WSL lacks spirv-reflect), same as the rest of the project.

against, so we build the cutting-edge version by construction, not aspiration.

## B8 — the frontier lighting + shadow system (gold standard)

> **Scope (user 2026-07-12): "full lit gold standard shadows and cascaded shadow maps… everything gold standard."** B8 is
> now the frontier RASTER renderer, not a seam-validation. It **amends ADR-0102 D7** (the "defer the full pipelines + light
> culling to the post-hesap rendering phase" boundary is superseded by the locked *full-visual-frontier-before-hesap* order —
> struck in place in ADR-0102). Two layers, both gold-standard, built together:
> **(1) the CKIR LIGHTING/SHADOW/SKINNING LIBRARY** — shader-side math authored in the IR (`ckir_lighting.hpp` + shadow/skin
> helpers), each bit-exact vs its published reference on the CPU oracle and observable pixel-identical on both backends, the
> exact B5–B7 methodology; **(2) the RHI RENDERER INTEGRATION** — the frame-graph passes (`FrameGraph`/`IRenderPath`, the
> reserved `PassType::Shadow`), the `MaterialTemplate`/`variant_for_pass` cook seam fed by `lower_entry` (B7), and the
> descriptor-set frequency model (ADR-0102 D2: set 0 frame · set 1 pass/lights/shadows · set 2 material · set 3 object/skin).

**The frontier technique stack (the gold-standard bar each sub-slice is measured against).** STATUS AUDIT 2026-07-15: **every
row's portable SHADER MATH is implemented in CKIR, bit-exact on the CPU oracle + pixel-identical on Vulkan AND DX12** (the tests in
the ✅ status column pass). Where a row says "→ render phase" that is the DEVICE/RENDER-PIPELINE plumbing (render-to-texture,
structured buffers, float/2DArray texture storage, cull-compute dispatch, VSM page-table + GPU-feedback allocation) — deferred
UNIFORMLY for every technique to the post-detour render phase per the detour charter (harden the IR + shader math now; wire the
real GPU pipeline later), NOT a per-technique shortcut. **No before-B16 shader technique is missing.** (Correcting a 2026-07-15
mis-audit: VSM **is** built — `vsm_clipmap_level`+`vsm_page_coord`, `[ssshadow]` bit-exact both backends.)

| capability | gold-standard technique | reference | sub-slice | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Specular BRDF | GGX/Trowbridge-Reitz `D` · height-correlated Smith `V` · Schlick `F` | Filament / Frostbite (Karis, Lagarde) | **B8-a** | ✅ math `[brdf]` |
| Energy conservation | multiscatter GGX energy compensation | Kulla-Conty; Fdez-Agüera | **B8-a** | ✅ math (+DFG-bias-floor correctness fix) |
| Diffuse | Lambert + Burley/Disney (retro-reflection) | Disney BRDF (Burley 2012) | **B8-a** | ✅ math `[brdf]` |
| Layered material | anisotropic GGX · clearcoat (IOR 1.5) · sheen `D_Charlie`/`V_Neubelt` · thin-film · transmission · subsurface | Filament; OpenPBR 1.1 (the B5 slab) | **B8-b** | ✅ math `[lobes]`/`[thinfilm]`/`[transmission]`; scene-colour sample → render phase |
| Punctual lights | directional · point (inverse-square + smooth window) · spot (cone) | Frostbite (Lagarde-de Rousiers 2014) | **B8-c** | ✅ math `[lights]`; light-array SSBO → render phase |
| **Area lights** | **Linearly Transformed Cosines** (rect/disk/sphere/tube) + LTC LUT; anisotropic-GGX LTC | Heitz 2016; Heitz-Dupuy 2022 | **B8-d** | ✅ math `[area]` (rect/tube/disk/sphere+aniso); LTC LUT texture → render phase |
| Image-based lighting | split-sum: prefiltered env (roughness mips) + DFG LUT + irradiance SH L2 | Karis 2013 (UE4 split-sum) | **B8-e** | ✅ math `[ibl]` (runtime+generation); env/DFG/SH assets → render phase |
| Shadow bias | normal-offset + slope-scaled + receiver-plane depth bias | MJP "A Sampling of Shadow Techniques" | **B8-f** | ✅ math `[shadow]` |
| Soft shadows | **PCF** (rotated Poisson/Gather) → **PCSS** (blocker search + penumbra, contact-hardening) | Fernando 2005 (PCSS); Nvidia | **B8-g** | ✅ math `[softshadow]` |
| Filterable shadows | **EVSM** · **Moment Shadow Maps** (4-moment, prefilter/MSAA) | Lauritzen (EVSM); Peters-Klein 2015 (MSM) | **B8-g** | ✅ math `[softshadow]` (resolve); float moment-map storage → render phase |
| **Cascaded shadow maps** | **stable CSM** (Valient sphere-bound + texel-snap) + **SDSM** tight fit + smooth cascade blend + per-cascade PCF/PCSS | Valient; Lauritzen 2011 (SDSM); MS "CSM" | **B8-h** | ✅ split/select/Valient-snap/blend `[csm]`; SDSM = feed the split fn the `ckir_reduce` depth-min/max range (reduction pass) + 2DArray atlas → render phase |
| Contact / fine shadows | screen-space ray-marched contact shadows | Bavoil; UE contact shadows | **B8-i** | ✅ math `[ssshadow]`; depth-buffer sample → render phase |
| Raster shadow ceiling | **Virtual Shadow Maps** (page-table/clipmap atlas, GPU-feedback allocation) | UE5 VSM | **B8-i** | ✅ addressing math `[ssshadow]` (`vsm_clipmap_level`+`vsm_page_coord`); page-table + feedback alloc → render phase |
| Skinning | linear-blend + **dual-quaternion** (no candy-wrapper) + optional compute pre-skin | Kavan 2007 (DQS) | **B8-j** | ✅ math `[skin]` (LBS+DQS); bone-palette SSBO + pre-skin dispatch → render phase |
| Lighting paths | **Forward+** (clustered froxel cull) + **Deferred** (G-buffer) HYBRID | ADR-0102 D3 (deferred opaque + forward transparent) | **B8-l** | ✅ math `[render]` (cluster/cull/G-buffer-decode); cull-compute dispatch + G-buffer RT → render phase |

**Ray-traced shadows / RTGI are NOT B8** — they are the highest-quality path but belong to **B9 + C3** (RT pipelines); B8 is
the frontier *raster* renderer. The lighting library it builds is reused by the RT path (one BRDF, two visibility sources).
**Neural** materials/BRDF are **B10**. B8's job is the complete, correct, gold-standard raster lighting + shadow system, with
the CKIR lighting library as the single shared surface→radiance evaluator both raster and (later) RT call.

## Stage model — complete from day one (B3-a′ ✅)

`ShaderStage` = the 14 SPIR-V execution models: `Compute · Vertex · TessControl · TessEval · Geometry · Fragment · Task ·
Mesh · RayGen · Intersection · AnyHit · ClosestHit · Miss · Callable`. Mesh/task is the amplification path; **geometry is
supported-but-discouraged** (single-thread bottleneck). **Tessellation (`TessControl`/`TessEval`) is a REAL path, not
emulated** (B4-tess): it is the portable displacement/terrain path for platforms without mesh shaders (mobile/WebGPU/older
HW), and its builtins (`TessCoord`, `PatchVertexCount`, `InvocationId`) are already in the stage model. A backend that cannot
yet emit a stage **refuses loudly** — it never silently falls back to compute. (Declare the whole domain; refuse the
unimplemented part loudly.) *Candidate (not scheduled): single-pass stereo / multiview (`VK_KHR_multiview` + a `ViewIndex`
builtin) — the frontier replacement for GS-based stereo; parked until VR/XR is a committed target.*

## Escape hatch (ADR-0101 §4) — preserved and boxed

Hand-written per-backend kernels stay legal (geometry-bvh-gpu's LBVH `.comp`, imgui/draw overlay GLSL, third-party HLSL):
they cook to blobs and load through `create_program(cooked)` — explicitly non-portable, never re-entering the engine as
source. The asset cooker keeps its GLSL→SPIR-V path; it just calls the *Vulkan backend's* compiler, not `crd-shader`.

## Front-ends (node editor + text DSL) — DEFERRED to the editor phase (NOT part of this detour)

> This is the old "D-007 Phase C" — renamed here so it stops colliding with the device C-slices above. It is **not** on the
> execution table; it is built at editor time (roadmap step 7). Authoring during this detour + the rendering phase is via
> the existing **C++ builders**.

Only the **round-trip design invariant is locked now** (cheap insurance that the IR is provably editable): every
KOp/builder/stage-construct maps to a typed nodedef, and graph→IR→graph is loss-less (validated by a decompile test). The
node-editor UI, the Slang-inspired text DSL, the shared semantic/LSP layer, and live-preview authoring all land in the
editor phase.

## Exit criteria

The detour exits — and the main roadmap resumes at **Phase 3.1.6 v17 hesap-GPU compute** — when the master table's rows
1–29 are ✅: the IR can express (and every validatable backend can run) ML/AI, FFT, simulation, skinning, particles,
lighting, PBR + stylized/NPR rendering, mesh shading, ray tracing, neural shading, and GPU-driven work — and the cook path
makes it shippable with zero runtime compile. Then: hesap-GPU → eylem/physics → rendering → UI → editor → node editor.
